## Session 2026-11-04 (108) — the eleventh panel, the truck's coach, and an expert as good as Sal (main, rules 11)

- **The test link serves 49e681e (rules 11)** since CI went green on it (ci and perf).
- **Panel round 11, cold** (`docs/baselines/fun/panel-r11/`, the same six personas as r8 to r10, tester build of 49e681e): fun 4, 4, 3, 4, 3, 4 (mean 3.67, median 4; r10 3.17), a game finished 5 of 6 (r10 3), a second game by choice 1 (2), back tomorrow yes 2, maybe 3, no 1 (r10 0, 5, 1), stuck twice each (12; r10 14). What it found and what changed is in DESIGN.md.
- **Fixed from it:** the coach plans the card picked as the truck comes on the yard with the truck (`buildStart` in `render/guide.ts`; a test that the coach's plan is the card tag's at the truck's shift, failing before the fix), and the summary's repeat line no longer says a machine short of scrap held the line "0 of 80 seconds".
- **The card's expert** (GOALS.md, "When a measure changes"): it scored below Sal on 12 of 40 held-out yards; it now also starts from Sal's line. Before, the same as `card-rules11.json`: 79, lowest 38 (skill 38, picks 54, ladder 100). After: **79, lowest 54**: alive 100, picks 54 (real picks 0.321, healthy cards 0.75: the Press taken 0.088 of the times offered), skill 56 (tiers in order 0.725, expert over novice 1.98, 8× the thinking +8.1 %), moving 73, ways 73, ladder 87 (novice1 0.9, competent2 0.875, expert3 0.8), one more try 100, climax 100, yards 77, forgiving 100. Expert median $18,482 (was $17,968). `tools/fun-card.ts --json` now writes each yard's four tiers (`rows`) and each ruined version's dimensions.
- **Checks:** unit 538 pass; browser suite 175 of 179 with three key-binding tests failing beside the others and passing alone (9 of 9, the known stall); lint, format, strings clean.
- **Goal 2 with the new expert** (`docs/baselines/fun/card-rules11b.json`, 807 s): the game 79, lowest 54; one card only -34, raw scrap -25, machines crawl -35, stars for nothing -16, two-second shifts -29, every yard alike -5 (Yards differ 77 to 15 under it; GOALS.md says why the weights stay). That file predates the card's tiers selling their spares.
- **Spare parts, the raise, Sal in plain words** (DESIGN.md, the eleventh panel): once a card's plan is in, the coach says its spare sells for $10 and offers "Sell Dock" beside it; Sal and the card's lit tiers sell the same parts at the same moment. The job card names the job's price after each order ("ingots sell for $24, then $30, then $36"), the banner and ribbon the new price. Sal's "never moves a machine" lines say what a player can do about it. A browser test sells a spare from the coach; checked at 390 px (the coach fits beside its button).

## Session 2026-11-02 (106) — a lighter first visit, a thousand random sessions, and where the real choices are (main)

- **Panel round 8, cold** (`docs/baselines/fun/panel-r8/`), six agents who had never seen the game, four on a phone and two at a desk, on the tester build of 7d8e37f: fun 4, 4, 3, 3, 3, 2 (median 3); clear 4 (median); three finished a game, none played another by choice; back tomorrow one yes, four maybe, one no; five were stuck at least once. Two ran out of the panel's 150 actions mid-game, and the slowest reader spent all fifteen minutes in the three lessons.
- **What they said, nearly in one voice:** four of six asked to decide for themselves ("make me decide something every shift instead of tapping where it glows", "stop lighting every square for me", "let me work something out for myself sooner", "a real decision from the first shift"), as one returning agent of round 7 did. Their best moments were the times they placed something themselves and the forecast agreed: a Crusher in front of a Plate Press, a Dock dragged away, a line rebuilt round a Splitter. Five of six met the Plate Press card saying "No easy spot yet" and did not learn why; one read it as "the yard is full" on a mostly empty yard.
- **Panel round 9, the same six with a game's spots dark until asked for** (`docs/baselines/fun/panel-r9/`): fun 3, 4, 4, 4, 3, 3 against round 8's 4, 3, 3, 4, 2, 3 (mean 3.17 to 3.5); a game finished 4 of 6 against 3; one played a second game by choice against none; back tomorrow six maybe; all six stuck at least once against five. Kept: `lightsOn` is off unless chosen, and the round's stuck moments are fixed (DESIGN.md, "the first game is the player's to work out"): "Show me where" no longer cut off at 360 to 412 pixels, the card and the coach say why no spot pays (`pressPaysLess`, `noSpotText`), a card's spare part waits without being asked for (`coach.readySpare`), the Tuner advice needs a side it could stand on (`roomForTuner` in `sim/bottleneck.ts`, advice only: no rule, no replay and no golden hash reads it), Buy a belt beside Start in the row that stays at a phone's foot, Turn right and Turn left, "Show where parts go: on/off", a Crusher turns its own card's Hopper, a part never faces a Hopper beside it with nothing to feed, and a note when a drop turns another part.
- **Panel round 10, the same six on c662f7d** (`docs/baselines/fun/panel-r10/`): fun 3, 3, 3, 3, 3, 4 (mean 3.17; rounds 8 and 9: 3.17 and 3.50); a game finished 3 of 6; a second game by choice 2 of 6 (0, then 1); stuck moments 14 (13, then 19). Fun is flat within the noise and the dark default stays; its fixes are in the next commit (DESIGN.md): `coach.shortFeed`, a loose piece only when items reach it, "held you back" from lesson 3, `job.salLevel` saying how Sal can be beaten.
- **On trial, a scrap truck at shift 4** (DESIGN.md): the card 75 against 74 on tune yards and 74 against 73 held out; blind comparison 4 (`docs/baselines/fun/blind-4/`), three of four returning agents chose it, but the free Hopper never earned in any game. Committed inert (`TRIAL.delivery` 0); the next version has to pay. The paying version (a Hopper with its Crusher, `TRIAL.deliveryKit`) with the late orders raised 15 %: 77 against 74 on tune yards, 74 against 73 held out.
- **The panel's windows:** every agent session opened a blank terminal window on the owner's screen, and closing one killed that session's browser: all four first sessions of blind comparison 4 reported "Target crashed", which 16 runs here (4 alone, 12 four at a time with screenshots) did not reproduce. `tools/panel/drive.mjs` now starts the browser from a helper with a hidden console: no window, nothing left after `stop`.
- **New yard beside Play again:** the results had it as a quiet link in the footer, below the sharing, and the tenth panel's expert, who wanted a second yard, never found it. It is in the play row now, and the footer no longer repeats it.
- **Goal 12, the third bar measured:** `tools/first-money.ts` plays the production build as a newcomer on the slow-4G phone: everything new on the screen read at 250 words a minute before the next touch, 0.8 s a touch, only what is lit touched, and the shift at its own speed. Money is on the top bar 22,388 ms after landing (runs 22,252, 22,388, 22,484; 17,040 ms of it reading and touching, 4 touches), and 34,946 ms for a reader at 150 words a minute and 1.5 s a touch. With 240.9 KiB and 1.9 s to a tappable title, all three of Goal 12's bars are met on this machine's measure; no gate in `perf.yml` was loosened.
- **The card and the sameness ruin** (Goal 2): Yards differ now also reads opening variety (`openingOf`, `openingVariety` in `tools/fun-lib.ts`). On 40 held-out yards with `--validate` (`tools/scratch/card-yards.log`, 905 s): the game 76.4 under the card's weights; the ruins −27.9, −22.1, −37.5, −15.2, −38.0 and −0.4 (every yard alike, +1 before). Tried and dropped: Yards at 15 (the ladder 10, one more try 5): every yard alike −6, stars for nothing −8. Goal 2 stays unmet on that ruin (GOALS.md).
- **CI noise:** c662f7d failed twice on two different server tests (friends, then a results screen whose read lost its preflight). The fixture now ignores a preflight with no usable answer, as it already did for play data; a reply without CORS headers still fails, and `tests/verifier.test.ts` holds the server's preflight answer.
- **Still asked for, every round:** lessons that decide nothing for ten minutes (the slowest reader spent all fifteen in them, twice; the lessons' content is the owner's), a score to chase once Sal is beaten (the test link has no board by design; a Sal who plans ahead is only 1.2 % better), and a way to beat Sal that a casual player can act on.
- **Fixed from it at once:** a Hopper put down faced a Tuner that led into a Furnace (scrap to slag, the forecast down $1,296). A Hopper now faces the way its scrap reaches a Crusher, then a Splitter or a Sieve, then a free cell or a Dock, and never a Furnace; a machine fed from one side goes straight on unless the way ahead is blocked (the side turn of the morning turned one agent's Furnace away from the cell he was building toward); and a Crusher dropped beside a Hopper turns that Hopper only when its scrap goes nowhere, into a free cell, or into a Furnace or a Press, so the starter line that sells raw scrap keeps working (the browser test that plays a round on moved keys caught that one).

- **Goal 11, the tapping half, measured for the first time:** `node --import tsx tools/monkey.ts browser` against the tester build of dadf52c (the panel site, its stand-in database apart from the panel's): 1,000 sessions of 60 random actions, no page error, 496 s.
- **CI on dadf52c:** the touch file's "rounds two to six by touch" timed out once, waiting 15 s for the next draft after Next; the rerun was green, and the file passed three times in three here. Noted in case it comes back: a tap that is lost would look like this. The test link was redeployed from dadf52c and answers with no page errors.
- **Where the real choices are** (`tools/scratch/pick-study.ts`, the card's tree on 24 tune yards): real picks by shift 42, 38, 29, 21, 21, 17 %. A clear winner is mostly the Hopper's kit in shifts 1 to 3 and the job's machine in shift 4; in shift 6, 46 % of picks do not matter. On an ingot yard, two in three, nothing new is worth building after the second buyer comes: a Press earns half of what a Furnace does per shard, which is the seventh panel's "the same move again".
- **Tried and set aside** (DESIGN.md): the other product's machine as a kit with its own Splitter and Dock, with the plate buyer at 100, 150 and 200 %. The card on 24 tune yards: 73, 73, 73 against 73, and plates at 200 alone 72. A better price makes one more clear winner, not a choice. `TRIAL` (outside `RULES`, so neither play nor the policy reads it) now holds trials; it is empty again.
- **Goal 12, both bars met here:** after WebGL (297.0 to 262.4 KiB), the two core files that required Phaser's whole Math namespace for its random generator get `game/phaser-math.cjs` instead (the 3D maths stayed out), and Groups, which the game never made, are out of `game/phaser-entry.cjs` with the Sprite and animation state they brought: 240.9 KiB gzip. The page now preloads the chunks a first visit needs (`<link rel="modulepreload">` from `build.json`'s list), because on slow 4G the 193 KiB shared chunk waited about 400 ms for the bundle to arrive and be read: first interactive 2,083 ms to 1,898 (runs 1,892, 1,898, 1,921). Browser suite 178 pass; deploy, service worker and hardening files 21 pass. "First money within 60 s of landing" is not measured by a tool yet.
- **Less turning:** the seventh panel's worst moment was "about ten clicks" turning a Hopper and a Crusher the game had pointed the wrong way. A Crusher put beside a Hopper that feeds nothing now turns that Hopper to feed it in the same edit (one Undo), and a machine fed from one side faces whatever takes its load instead of straight on into nothing (`smartFacing`, `dropActions` in `render/placement.ts`, two new tests).

## Session 2026-11-02 (105) — the seventh panel on rules 10 (main, no rules change)

- **Panel round 7**, four returning players on rules 10 (`docs/baselines/fun/panel-r7/`): fun 4, 4, 3, 3; clear 4, 4, 3, 3; two played another game by choice; back tomorrow two yes and two maybe (round 6 on the same day's yard under rules 9: four maybe). **Blind comparison 3** (`docs/baselines/fun/blind-3/`): both chose rules 10, fun 3 against 2 and 4 against 3, in opposite play orders.
- **What changed:** games and an earlier best on Home count under any rules (`gamesEver`); Sal sells nothing (the $10 a spare kit Dock fetched left lights-followers exactly $10 behind); `coach.placeFreeTouch` on a touch screen. A tap on the yard placing the spare part was tried and reverted: it broke the touch test's rule that a stray tap only lets go.
- **Then, from the same round:** ready but short of the order, the coach says by how much and lights the slow machine (`coach.shortSlow`, `coach.shortStarved`); Turn back, a quarter turn the other way as one edit; a burst of colour on the results for beating Sal or filling every order (`cheer`, none with reduced motion).
- **A slip of mine:** 4a70c56 went out after four browser files instead of all of them, and the records test, which still expected "No finished games yet" for a profile with only older records, failed in CI. The next commit updates it; the full suite passes.
- **The crowded shelf, measured:** played Sal's way under rules 10, the shelf held more than seven parts in every one of 60 games (median peak 9; games ended with 4.5 belts and 2.1 Docks on it). Two spare belts to start and a card's Dock going back when the shift starts took that to 3 of 60, but cost real picks on the card (29 % to 24 %, total −1), so they were not adopted. Instead belts share one slot on the shelf with a count (`shelfSlotsOf`, `setShelf` in `render/layout.ts`; the lit spots and the tests' geometry speak in slots).
- **The planner as a target, measured and dropped:** the card's tree search, worked out ahead for each dated yard, would have been a second line to chase, but at budget 200 it found $13,070 on the day's yard where three agents made $16,023, $20,334 and $23,644. The card's expert tier sits well below strong players, which is worth knowing when reading its Skill measure.
- **Goal 12, first visit:** it had crept to 297.0 KiB gzip of the 300 budget. WebGL is out of the build (`WEBGL_RENDERER: 'false'`, `type: Phaser.CANVAS`): 262.4 KiB. The three tints the game used (the stall mark, the transform flashes, a Press holding a shard) are drawn once on canvases (`render/tint.ts`), the WebGL context-loss test went with WebGL, and preset B is measured on Canvas only, its Canvas baseline unchanged and its WebGL sample removed.
- **Still open:** a try's better score goes on no board (the owner's decision, minded by two agents); experts find Sal too easy once the wall is gone; first visit is still 12 KiB over Goal 12's 250.

## Session 2026-11-02 (99) — rules 7: orders, a running start, kits, lit spots, Sal (main)

- **Rules 6, then 7, on main.** A job is six orders in money. Fill one and every price rises 25 % for the rest of the game; stars are orders filled. Every generated yard opens with its line running, a Hopper card brings a Crusher, the job's machine brings a Splitter, a second buyer comes in from shift 4, machines take from the sides, and the shelf opens with four belts. `docs/DESIGN.md` has the evidence.
- **The card on the same 40 held-out yards:** rules 5 **55**, lowest 23. Rules 6 **77**, lowest 50, ruins −22, −30, −47, −11, −38, −3. Rules 7 with the honest tiers **72**, lowest 35: Alive 99, Pick 35, Skill 36, Moving 60, Ways 80, Ladder 93, One more try 100, Climax 100, Yards 60, Forgiving 100, medians $7,776, $10,452, $11,823, $13,614, ruins −38, −22, −42, −14, −37, −6. Two ruins are still under the bar of 15.
- **Lit spots.** The card's estimate now hands the coach its placements, one lit spot at a time, with the facing right. Every plan, made with the game's own moves on 60 yards, earned exactly what it said.
- **What that exposed:** a player who took the biggest figure and followed the lights scored $11,310 against the search's $11,736, three stars on 23 of 24 yards. So a card says whether it helps and not how much, that player became Sal's game (the target the panel asked for), and the orders were re-priced so three stars takes more than the lights. A search eight times deeper beats Sal by 13 to 29 %.
- **Also:** the results screen loads when a game ends, first visit 293.8 KiB; money has its comma; the balance tool's tests left the coverage run, where they had stopped finishing, for `test:timing`.
- **Panel round 3 on rules 7** (eight agents, `docs/baselines/fun/panel-r3/`): median fun 3, clear 4, six finished a game, tomorrow one yes and seven maybe, all eight stuck at the same places: lit spots that stopped at shift 4, advice repeated after it was taken, a red mark with no mark, a results line that did not add up, a Furnace with no free side for a Tuner.
- **Rules 8 answered it** (`docs/DESIGN.md`): a Tuner carries what a belt carries and speeds a Hopper, a belt piece sits between the Crusher and the job's machine, a pick can be taken back until the shift starts, and the orders moved with them. Also: the lit spot takes one tap, Move, an order filled stops the eye, a try from the first order missed, Lit spots on or off, Sal's number on Home, and the verifier plays Sal's game for the foreman's mark. A rush job card was tried on the card and lost (−2 to −4), so it went.
- **The card on rules 8** (40 held-out yards, `docs/baselines/fun/card-rules8.json`): **72, lowest 29**. Alive 100, Pick 29, Skill 32, Moving 61, Ways 97, Ladder 100, One more try 100, Climax 72, Yards 64, Forgiving 94; ruins −38, −21, −39, −15, −32, −0. The card barely moves with changes a person feels, which is why the panel runs beside it.

The worker holds its write open with `waitUntil` because Cloudflare documents that a caller hanging up cancels a request; no measurement here shows it helping.

## Session 2026-11-01 (89) — the perf gate, measured: the yardstick was wrong (main, tools only, no rules change)

- **Session 86's idea failed, and the numbers say so.** Six runs of identical code on different runners, read from their artifacts: the calibrated load varied more than raw total script time on every sample (WebGL 36 % against 10 %, Canvas 15 % against 11 %, preset D 20 % against 13 %), and the yardstick did not track script time at all (correlation −0.74, 0.64, 0.31). It swung 2× by itself. It is gone. One comparison on a single rerun had looked good, and I said so too early.
- **What the data did support:** the whole run's script time beats per frame on every sample, because at a fixed game speed a slow host draws fewer frames and each does more. `tools/perf-gate.ts` now reads that.
- **Hosts still differ, so a baseline comes from several runs.** `tools/perf-baseline.ts` takes the sample with the middle total for each label and the worst drop rate seen, so a slow host passes on identical code. The committed baselines come from the twenty green runs on disk, and preset D has one for the first time, which the workflow's comment had been waiting for.
- **Replayed over all forty reports:** 39 pass and 1 false alarm, on preset D, which now has the two-run rule too. With every report's script time doubled, the gate catches 38 of 40; the two it misses are the fastest hosts on preset D. So it fails clean commits rarely and catches a doubling nearly always, which neither was true before. It will not catch a 30 % regression reliably, and nothing short of measuring two builds on the same host will. That is the real limit of this gate.
- **For a rendering change:** run a green main run a few times, download the artifacts, feed them to the tool. Rerunning one run is the cheapest way to meet several hosts.
- GitHub on 8859440, which carries c06fb14, d53e8db and dadb8b3: ci 41296020885 and perf 41296021022 green on the first attempt. That is the new gate's first real run: script 706 ms against a baseline of 776 on WebGL, 1135 against 1110 on Canvas, 1325 against 1428 on preset D. First visit 295.5 KiB gzip, first interactive 2750 ms there.
- Verified: `tests/perf-gate.test.ts` 6 pass, the replay above. **Every routine item on the roadmap is done.** What is left needs the owner, a person or a commission: the list is the ⚑ lines in `docs/ROADMAP.md`, and the first decision on it is the weekly digest in session 85.

## Session 2026-11-01 (88) — production roadmap 0.12.5 verifier operations (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

- **Metrics.** `GET /metrics` gives what `/health` gives as OpenMetrics text: counts, sizes and durations, nothing that names anyone. It exists only with `METRICS_TOKEN` and answers only to it. CI starts the image with a token and `tools/image-smoke.ts` scrapes it once, reads the entry back and checks a stranger is refused.
- **Runbooks** in `server/README.md`: an incident, a leaked secret with what each lets someone do and what rotating it costs, a request about someone's data, a day gone wrong, and a restore. The patch cadence for Node, Electron, dependencies and Phaser is beside them. The SQLite decision is recorded again: no beta month has run, so the trigger has not been crossed.
- **Signals.** Migration 6. A game on a daily or a weekly notes when it arrived, how many tries that was, and whether someone else already holds the same game. That last case is the one worth having, and the first version missed it: an identical game is refused in the cheap check before any simulation, so it is noted from there. `server/review.ts` and `tools/review.ts` read them for a person. Nothing acts on a signal. They age out after 90 days and go with the player. The client's think time per action, which PLAN-v1 named, is not recorded: the game sends no clock, and nothing needs one.
- **Two boards.** A game sent with `origin: tool` keeps a board of its own. `rank()` is what every board, medal, chart and division reads, so one filter there keeps it off all of them. A player keeps one best on each. A production page with the test hooks open tags itself; a dev build does not, because the browser tests read the people's board. `POST /moderation/reclassify` moves a game either way with a reason in the audit log; a closed day is final.
- **Voiding a day.** `tools/void-day.ts` says what it would do and needs `--yes`; only the owner's token will do. The games leave the board for `voided/<date>-<n>.json`, what the day earned is taken back, and the day starts again on a new yard with a full window. `tools/mint-check.ts` still passes, because the day before committed to the voided seed and the new record names it; a test runs it. A kept file is held to the same promises as the rest: it is in the export, and forgetting a player reaches it. My first version did neither, and the test I wrote for it said so.
- **Waiting for the owner:** the dashboard screenshot needs a dashboard, which goes with the hosting ⚑.

## Session 2026-11-01 (87) — production roadmap 0.12.4 accessibility completion (main, no rules change, protocol 3)

- **Keys move.** Settings, Keys lists every key of every action; click one, press the new one. A slot in the action table is one key of one action, and a handler goes on hearing its slot's own key, so moving a key changes what fires an action and nothing else. A key that already does something else is refused by name, not swapped behind the player's back. Esc lets go, one button restores the usual keys, and the Tips page and the keyboard line say the keys that work. Kept on this device: a keyboard belongs to the machine in front of you.
- **The yard reads.** `render/mirror.ts`, pure, gives a hidden list a line for each machine (where, what, which way it faces, whether its way out goes anywhere, the same answer the flow overlay gives), each rubble spot and each part on the shelf. The keyboard's cursor says where it is in a live region, and the foreman's lines already were one.
- **A bug that has been there all along, and only a real key test found it.** Phaser replays its whole key queue on each new event until the frame ends, and its duplicate check only compares against the last event, so three events in one frame fire the earlier keydowns again. Two keys pressed quickly moved the cursor five times. At 30 frames a second one quick double tap is enough to skip a cell or turn a machine twice. A comment in `InputController` had blamed Chromium and guarded only undo and redo with a 30 ms window; the real fix is that each binding hears an event once, and the window is gone.
- **What the focus audit found.** `tests/access-e2e.test.mjs` walks Tab at 390 px over Home, Settings, Keys, More, the paint shop, Privacy, the game, its Menu and the results: everything a mouse can reach is reached, on screen, with a ring. It found three things. In a game Tab is the shelf, so a keyboard could not reach the Menu at all: F6 now goes to the buttons and back, a new row in the action table. `choiceDialog` and `dialog` each kept their own Tab list, which skipped anything else in the dialog, including the Beat this and Add friend I had just put on the profile: one trap in `ui.layer` now holds for every dialog. And no other panel kept focus in at all.

## Session 2026-11-01 (86) — the perf gate gets a yardstick (main, tools only, no rules change)

- Session 82 left this open: the gate compared raw script time against a baseline from another machine. Fourteen green runs of `perf`, read from their artifacts, say how much that matters: on identical code the Canvas sample ranged from 3.40 to 8.39 ms per frame (2.5×) and the WebGL sample from 7.70 to 16.72 (2.2×). A slow host failed clean commits, which is what happened on 62bc458, and a fast one could have hidden a doubled workload.
- Per frame was the wrong number as well as a raw one: at a fixed game speed a slow host draws fewer frames, so each does more. The whole run's script time varied less, 1.6× to 1.9×.
- `tools/perf.ts` now times a fixed workload in the page at the same throttle, before and after the sample (`calibrationMs`), and `tools/perf-gate.ts` compares the whole run's script time over it (`scriptLoad`) when both sides carry it. A baseline without it falls back to the old rule, so this commit changes nothing in CI by itself. Three runs here read 49.0, 49.3 and 50.0.
- **Not armed yet, and not proven on the runners.** It takes a baseline from CI artifacts with `calibrationMs` in them, and the spread across real hosts is the number that says whether this worked. Both come from the next few runs.
- Browser tests no longer count a failed play-data `POST /events` as a page error: a batch that fails goes back in its queue and a player sees nothing. That is what failed a9c0ac5's first attempt.
- Verified: `tests/perf-gate.test.ts` 5 pass, `tests/render-e2e.test.mjs` 20 pass with the new fixture, the calibrated tool run for real on preset D. Next: the baseline, then 0.12.4.

## Session 2026-11-01 (84) — production roadmap 0.11.12 cosmetics (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

- `game/src/content/cosmetics.ts` is the one list both sides read: four yard colours, four machine paints, five titles, thirteen in all. The server decides what is earned from days of dailies and trophies (`earnCosmetics`, run from `/me/divisions` when Home asks and from `/me/cosmetics`); the table has been there since 0.11.0, so there is no migration. A row stays once written, so a later change to the numbers takes nothing away, and each new thing gets one line in the inbox. **The numbers and the names are mine, not the owner's:** first yard colour at 3 days, first paint at 5, first title at 7, the rest at 10 and 30 days or at medals, gold and days in the top division. They are one line each to change.
- Players read **paint shop**, in More on Home and from the inbox line: Yard, Machines, Title, what is in use, and for the rest what it takes and how far along you are. It opens with nothing earned too, so a new player can see what there is. A device with no key yet sees the list at zero; with no server it says it cannot open.
- **How paint is drawn:** the roadmap says placeholder tints, but Phaser's `setTint` does nothing on the Canvas renderer, which is what weak phones run. Every sprite key is an alias onto the shared atlas, so `render/paint.ts` swaps the aliases of the `m-*` frames, or `ground` and `shelf`, for recoloured copies on a canvas when a Game scene starts, using the `color` blend so each pixel keeps its lightness and a machine keeps its shape. The alias keeps its key, so nothing else in the game knows: the board, the shelf, the offers and the GIF all ask for the same keys. Materials are never painted. A static test holds `game/src/sim` to importing none of it.
- **Shown to others:** the title on `/board`, `/daily`, `/weekly`, on a post and on a profile, in `withTitle` on Home, the results and the talk; the whole look on `GET /replay`, so a recording plays in its author's paint and the next game in your own. A run from an earlier device shows its player's. A hidden profile shows no title, and a title this client does not know says nothing, so a newer server cannot break it.
- `tests/paint-e2e.test.mjs` compares the frames themselves: Sam, in plain paint, watches Ann's recording and his machines and yard are exactly Ann's, then the next game is exactly his own. The Canvas renderer is checked from the pixels it drew.
- **Two things this turned up on the way.** While a recording played, the top bar cut its label to "recording by…", so you could not see whose game it was: Sal's line now says it whole. And a pasted recording's label was the literal string `replay playback` from `RoundController`, a banned word on a player's screen that the lint cannot see, because it reads `game/src/ui` and `game/src/content`: it is a string now.
- **Worth knowing:** a player alone on a day takes that day's gold, because fewer than 16 players make one division (0.11.11). So in the first weeks medals, and what they unlock, come easily. That is a fact of a small population, not a bug, and it stops by itself. Nothing stops anyone playing for days alone either: paint for days is meant to reward turning up.
- The profile's badge, which 0.11.13 left for this, is in. The colours are placeholders until the commission (0.12.0); the ids and the rules stay.

## Session 2026-11-01 (83) — production roadmap 0.11.13 the author card (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

- `GET /card?hash=…|postId=…` answers with a player's name, the month they arrived, trophies and three best games, one per yard. `ui/PlayerCardPanel.ts` opens it from a row of the board, a comment and a hub post. The player-facing word is **profile**: "card" already means the share picture in the code (`render/card.ts`, the `card.*` strings), and to a newcomer next to a score it means nothing. The server and the column keep the roadmap's word.
- **The switch:** Show my profile in Privacy & data (`POST /me/card`, on `accounts.card_private`, which 0.11.0 added and nothing read until now). Off, the answer is exactly `{ ok, player, friendable, private }`, whoever asks, on all three surfaces. The owner still sees theirs and is told the others do not. A player with no email can use it too: the row holds no `email_hash`, and being linked reads that, not the row.
- A block reads exactly like the switch, field for field, so it is not announced. Add friend stays on a private profile: it is an action, not something shown about them, and the recording offers it anyway.
- **What a card must not show:** a game on an open day stays off it until the day has closed or the viewer has played that yard, because the games are links into the reveal gate. A post that is down, taken back or behind its gate names nobody.
- **A bug this found, older than the card:** a run from before a device signed in is stored under that device's id, so `verifier.ownerOf` named the device. The switch would have missed those runs, `own` was false on your own old game, and Add friend from one asked a player who does not exist. `runOwner` in `createServer` resolves through `device_keys`, and every caller there now uses it. A card reads the games of every device a player has.
- **Cost of a public page:** `ColdStore.entriesOf` reads every month file, fine behind a key and rate limit for an export and not for a page anyone can ask for. `ColdStore.bestOf` builds an index of each player's top games once per change of the files. An old game whose recording was not kept is found by its yard, so `fetchChallenge` takes an optional seed.
- A post tells the panel whether it has a card (`postView.card`), so a deleted author is plain text. A board row uses `playerId`, which brings up the next point.
- **A correction to 0.11.8.** STATUS, CLAUDE.md and `tests/friends.test.ts` say no player id leaves the server. That holds for the community routes. `/board`, `/daily` and `/weekly` have carried `playerId` since 0.6.0 (`tests/verifier.test.ts` reads it). It is a hash of the device key, not the key, and a handle or a card resolves nothing from it, so nothing is exposed beyond a stable name for a board entry, but the sentence was too wide. CLAUDE.md now says what is true. Stripping it from public boards would mean moving reports to the hash alone; not done, and nothing depends on doing it.
- **A slip of mine, caught before it left this machine:** the new client module was first written to `game/src/render/card.ts`, which is the share card from 0.7.4. The tool said "updated" rather than "created", and git still had the file. It was restored unchanged and the new code went to `render/playercard.ts`. Nothing of it reached a commit.

Afterwards they are known by `friendHandle`, a hash of both ids that means something only to its viewer, so no player id leaves the server. Asking needs an email on both sides and a name.

Bounded twice: `ACTIVITY_PER_PLAYER` 50 lines per player and `FEED_MEMBERS` 300 players per reader, held by a test. A post on a yard the reader may not read yet stays out until they can, and one that came down leaves. Names come at read time.
- Friends show on the chart of scores: their bars in blue and a line naming them. `/histogram` needed the key for that, which it did not send unless a crew board was chosen.
- Ways in: Add friend on a recording, on a post and on the results of a challenge you played. Crews reach each other through the recordings on their boards. Block this player sits in the report dialog on a post.

## Session 2026-11-01 (78) — production roadmap 0.11.5 the day's talk (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

The first thing the moderation pipeline carries. Shut with writing: with no key Home is unchanged and `/thread` answers 503.

- `GET /thread`, `POST /posts/edit`, `POST /posts/delete`, `/community/day/<date or week>`. One thread per daily and weekly. The reveal gate covers the words as it covers the recordings: 403 on an open day without a checked game, 200 once there is one or the day has closed, and the web page of an open day shows nothing of its talk. No player id leaves the server, and a post's state reaches only its author.
- **Sal opens and closes each day.** `game/src/content/sal-posts.ts`: the opening is neutral and chosen from the day, the closing is a pure function of the board (runs, median, top three, the most reacted game; a tie over reactions goes to the better game). The server writes both, because a thread is also a web page, so they sit apart from the table the game loads and the verifier does not carry 990 strings. `tests/strings.test.ts` holds them to the glossary. The closing post is built on reading, at most once a minute, so the names in it follow renames and hides.
- **The panel** (`ui/ThreadPanel.ts`): posts, Sal, the box. A post shows at once to its author with "Checking…" and reads again three seconds later. Change for five minutes: the new words wait down and go back through the filter and the classifier, and a person's clearance of the old words lapses, so clean words cannot be swapped for bad ones. Take back: the words go at once, unless the post is down for a person. Report, attach my game, Watch. A device with no email is shown Sign in, one with no name is told, and a mute says until when.
- The way in exists only when the server says so (`talk` on `/daily`, `/weekly` and a checked `/verify`), so nothing changes while writing is off: Today's talk in Home's footer, with the count, and Talk about this yard on the results.
- Whoever else has words up in a thread gets one unread line per thread however much arrives (`thread-reply`), never for their own words and never for a post that came down. That is the "reply" of 0.11.7 for a flat thread.
- **A first-visit bug in my own design, found by measuring:** the panel took the first visit from 297.5 to 299.0 KiB for almost no code of its own. Each lazy `import()` makes esbuild cut what it shares with the game into chunks by which panels reach it: 13 files, ten of them under 5 KiB, each compressing alone. All the panels now load through one entry (`ui/lazy.ts`) and the two long texts through an `import()` of their own, which shares nothing: 3 files, **295.4 KiB**. Opening any panel now loads them all, about 10 KiB gzip, which is cheap and then cached. CLAUDE.md's Phaser rule says a screen goes behind `import()` like `ui/panels.ts`: that now means adding it to `ui/lazy.ts`.
- The verifier bundle is 315 KB unminified after the queue page and the talk, so `tests/verifier.test.ts` now holds it under 400 KB; the check exists to catch Phaser getting pulled in.
- `tests/e2e/standins.mjs` is the shared stand-in for the classifier and the mail service, and signs a page in through the game.

## Session 2026-11-01 (77) — production roadmap 0.11.2 the moderation pipeline (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

The owner said to build it switched off until a key exists. It is complete and dormant: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `MODERATION_TOKEN` open writing, and without them `POST /posts` answers 503 and the game is unchanged.

- `server/moderation.ts` is the one way free text reaches the store. **The filter** refuses at write time: blocked words on the folded form, whole words, so "therapist" passes; links; emails; phone numbers; home addresses; over 280 characters; the same words twice in ten minutes. `foldName` now reads Cyrillic and Greek look-alikes, for names too.
- **The classifier**: `claude-haiku-5-5` through one `fetch`, not the SDK, by rule 6 and because the verifier ships as one file. Fixed rubric, structured output, low effort, the post escaped so it cannot close its own tag, no name or id sent. Up to three tries for 408, 409, 429, 5xx and 529. A refusal, a cut-off answer, anything outside the schema counts as no answer. The token count of the rubric is an estimate until a live run.
- **Three bands**, set by the owner on the queue page: under 40 up; from 40 up and queued; from 80 down until a person looks. A post waits down for the answer, a few seconds, and its author reads it meanwhile. No answer, or the daily budget spent: up and queued, category `unread`. Anyone at risk reaches a person whatever the score.
- **The owner away is automatic**: a post waiting 48 hours drops the hide bar to 60 until the queue is reviewed. A switch would be forgotten on the way to the airport.
- Reports on posts: two established players, or one trusted moderator, take a post down until a person looks. A post a person cleared stays up, and new reports put it back in the queue.
- **The queue page** at `/moderation/` is compiled in like the migrations, holds no data, and runs under a policy of its own files only. Players' words are only ever text, which `tests/moderation.test.ts` greps for. Token, or a trusted moderator's key or recovery code. Clear, hide, put back, remove, mute 24 h, mute 7 d, ban, with a reason code and a private note; the appeals; reported names; the latest decisions with their undo; thresholds, usage and the role for the owner. Reads on a 390 px phone.
- Decisions on top of the roadmap's text, for safety: only the owner bans, changes the numbers or names moderators; nobody decides about themselves, and a moderator cannot answer an appeal against their own decision; a ban takes the moderator role; writing needs a display name as well as the email. An overturned ban puts back what it took down; a ban lifted as mercy leaves those posts down, to be put back one by one.
- Appeals from the inbox: one try, 30 days, while the decision stands, through the same filter; undone or kept, the player hears either way. A decision on a post answers any open appeal on it. The roadmap's `upheld` count is published as "decisions kept" beside "undone", because "upheld" reads either way.
- A ban holds the player and the hash of the address (`banned_emails`), so neither signing out nor deleting lifts it; the privacy notice says so. The words of a removed post go after 90 days. `DELETE /me` takes the appeal's words and closes it; the decision stays with no name. `/me/export` leaves out who decided and the moderator's note.
- `GET /transparency` feeds the fairness page's monthly counts, repeated for a minute because counting walks every post. Community guidelines v1, the privacy notice (Anthropic as a recipient, what a ban keeps), the terms and a "Writing and moderation" section on the fairness page. `deploy/README.md` §8 is the owner's checklist; `tools/moderation-corpus.ts --live` is the one thing that calls the real service.

## Session 2026-10-31 (76) — production roadmap 0.11.1 sign in with email (main, no rules change, protocol 3: additive)

The owner chose Resend after a comparison (free plan 3,000 a month and 100 a day, $20 for 50,000; Postmark is the fallback if the mail lands in spam, Cloudflare's sending is still in beta, Amazon SES is more setup than a solo project needs).

- `server/link.ts`: the address only as SHA-256, the link's token only as a hash; a link works once, for fifteen minutes; `signInMail`; `resendMailer` is one HTTPS POST with `fetch`, so no new dependency. `RESEND_API_KEY` turns it on; without it `/link/start` answers 503 and `/health` says `email: false`.
- Three decisions on top of the roadmap's text, for safety. **The token travels in the URL fragment**, so it reaches no server log. **Nothing happens until a person presses Sign in**, because mail scanners follow links. **The device that opens the link is the one that signs in, never the one that asked**, so a stranger who types your address and hopes you click gains nothing.
- **A device keeps its own key.** The alternative was to hand a second device the first one's key, which means the server holding keys. `device_keys` (migration 2) says which player the hash of a key stands for, and every route reads the bearer through `bearerPlayer`. `DELETE /me` also deletes what those keys had put on the boards under their own id, and `/me/export` shows it under `earlierDevices`. Signing out forgets the address and the copy; the device asking stays the player, the others go back to being themselves.
- The same answer for a known and an unknown address. Limits: 3 mails an hour per address, 5 per player, 10 per network address.
- Client: `render/link.ts`, `render/sync.ts` (pure merge: the higher best, the union of days, the newest standing, machine counts take the higher side because adding would count a game twice at every meeting), `ui/EmailLink.ts` behind `import()`, Settings → Email. Home brings a signed-in device level and redraws if it learnt something; the results send up what a game earned. Text size and picture quality stay on the device. Bests, stars, days, medals, standings, watched counts and machine counts travel: that is everything Your record reads.
- Writing, messaging and the hub read `CommunityStore.isLinked` when they arrive (0.11.5 on).
- The address hash is a plain SHA-256, as the roadmap decided. A stolen database would let someone test whether a known address plays here. A keyed hash would close that at the price of a secret that signs everyone out if lost; recorded in `server/README.md`, left as decided.
- Found by reading my own code after it passed: a device that signs in has no local game history, so it would have shown no inbox and no division line although the account has both. `hasInbox` now counts being signed in, and the e2e holds it. Keyboard focus was lost after a failed send, so Esc did nothing; fixed.
- Stale text corrected: the privacy summary, the fairness page and the README all said there were no accounts; the notice's "Not collected" line; `legalUpdated` moved to 2026-10-31. `deploy/README.md` §7 is the owner's checklist.
- Looked at on a 390 px phone: Settings, the request, the sent state, the confirmation. Clean, no overflow. The mail text is plain and short.
- GitHub on bca893d: `ci` 41233928325 green (test, engines, image) and `perf` 41233928287 green. The bundled verifier and the Docker image both start with the new migration.
- Verified on this tree: typecheck, lint, format:check, the strings lint and policy:check clean; npm test: unit 318 pass (coverage 96.71 / 91.02 / 93.16), the three timing tests pass uninstrumented, browser 97 pass, 1 skipped (Firefox locally), 0 fail. First interactive 2429 ms against 4000.
- **What a first visit loads is 298.3 KiB gzip against 300: 1.7 KiB left.** The string table is the main weight. Before 0.11.2 adds a queue and threads, strings used only by panels behind `import()` have to move into those chunks, or something else has to go.
- ⚑ Not verified: real delivery. No mail has gone through Resend and none can until the domain exists: Gmail, Outlook and iCloud are on the checklist. Next: 0.11.2 the moderation pipeline, which needs the owner's API budget and thresholds.

## Session 2026-10-31 (72) — production roadmap 0.11.0 the community store, and a first visit on a slow line (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- `server/community.ts`: `CommunityStore` on `node:sqlite` (loaded on first use through `process.getBuiltinModule`, so tools that import `server/verify.ts` print no ExperimentalWarning), WAL, one file next to the board (`COMMUNITY_DB`). `server/migrations/0001-community.ts` makes the roadmap's fifteen tables. **Numbered SQL modules, not loose .sql files as the roadmap said:** with files beside the bundle the verifier stopped being one file, and the release artifact, the rollback rehearsal and the image each had to carry a folder; compiled in, a build cannot ship without its migrations. Each migration runs in one transaction and is recorded with a hash; a changed one or a gap stops the start; a database newer than the code runs as it is, so migrations must only add. `threadFor`, `addPost`, `threadPage` (a cursor on `posts_page`), `exportPlayer`, `forget`, `health` (size and schema every time, counts at most once a minute, because `/health` skips the read limiter).
- `PLAYER_COLUMNS` is the one list `DELETE /me` and `/me/export` run on; `tests/community.test.ts` walks the schema so a new column has to be placed. Rows others read stay without a name (a hub topic, a closed division); the moderation log stays; the export leaves out link tokens and other people's blocks.
- `tools/backup-lib.ts`: `community.db` goes in as a `VACUUM INTO` snapshot taken while the verifier runs (a plain copy of a live WAL file can tear), its `-wal` and `-shm` never; `restoreDrill` checks `PRAGMA integrity_check` and that the restored service reports the archive's posts. `tools/community.ts <data-dir> status|down <version>`. `node:sqlite` needs Node 24 here; `engines` already said so.
- Nothing a player can reach writes to the store yet; the protocol stays 2 until the first route (0.11.3).

## Session 2026-10-31 (71) — the owner's first playtest (main, rules v4, protocol 2: additive)

The owner played the game for the first time and sent twelve notes, ending "Let's start with these changes and then continue". All twelve are in. What each one became:

1. **"The sounds sound like midi files"**: every sound is a recipe rendered in the browser (`game/src/sound/`, wood, felt, soft metal, no square waves), shipped goods play a soft melody from the shift's scale; `docs/SOUND.md`, Settings → Sound test. ⚑ Measured, not heard: nobody here has ears.
2. **"Unable to see what the pieces on the board do"**: tap anything in any phase (`ui/Inspector.ts`, `InputController.tap`): what it is, what goes in, what comes out, what it did last shift. A drag during the pick is told why not, a click is not.
3. **"Define an end goal at the start"**: every yard is a job (rules v4): ship 160 ingots, three stars, on a job card at the start, a bar with star marks in the HUD, a moment as each mark passes, and on the results exactly what the next star would have taken. Total stars climb a rank ladder on this device. The job's material pays $24, so the job and the board pull the same way. Calibration in `tools/job-study.ts`, `docs/baselines/0.11/job-study-v4.json`: the plain recipe earns exactly two stars on all 60 yards, the third needs a widened line. ⚑ This overrides the roadmap's "no contracts" (`docs/DESIGN.md`, PRODUCTION-ROADMAP §1): the owner asked for it in so many words.
4. **"Only ONE is selectable"**: the coach says so, the other two cards leave the screen, a card the job needs wears a badge.
5. **"90 degrees misaligned"**: the panel's picture faces the way the machine faces, and a drop on the belt takes its place facing the way the line runs (`render/placement.ts`).
6. **"A 90 degree turn conveyor"**: conveyors turn corners by themselves: one fed only from its side draws as a corner (`beltShape`), which was always legal; spare conveyors are on the shelf from the first shift. Presentation only.
7. **"NES quality"**: a sprite sheet drawn in code (`tools/make-art.mjs`, `docs/ART.md`) with materials, corner belts, junk piles, ground, a hero picture, and bundled fonts (Inter, Barlow Condensed), which also ends the layout differences between this PC and the CI runner. ⚑ The owner's eyes judge it.
8. **"The recommendations are not captured / what's the point?"**: one instruction at a time, with what to touch lit (`render/guide.ts`); last shift's advice stays on screen through the pick and the build with Show me; the lesson rebuilt from a gap in the belt; `tests/walkthrough-e2e.test.mjs` plays it touching only what is lit, by mouse and by thumb.
9. **"A way to end the tutorial", "Title is 2003"**: Skip the lesson is always on screen; Menu in a game, Home instead of the title (one primary action, today's job, your rank, today's board), Settings in a dialog, the results simplified. `docs/UX-STANDARD.md` is the written bar, and the banned-word lint now fails on round, title screen, submit, grid, cell, import and the rest; every player-facing string was rewritten to it.
10. **"Share stats by default"**: play data on unless turned off, one switch in Settings; a browser sending Global Privacy Control or Do Not Track starts with it off; the privacy notice says so (`tools/legal-lib.ts`, legitimate interest). ⚑ A stored install id may need consent for visitors in the EU and UK; that is the owner's call with a lawyer's eye, the default stays as asked until then.
11. **"Random Yard always produces the same board"**: literally true: the starter never used the seed. A yard's name now generates its belt, junk piles and two spare conveyors (`game/src/sim/yard.ts`); `classic-*` seeds keep the old starter for tests with fixed coordinates.
12. Rules v4: `RULES_VERSION` 4, golden corpus regenerated with three new yard replays (15 replays), v3 kept byte for byte under `tests/golden/archive/v3/`, policy and legal regenerated; the verifier stamps `stars` on entries (additive, protocol 2).

Enabled only with VAPID keys.
- `server/verify.ts`: `ServerOptions.push`; `GET /push/vapid` (503 without keys), `POST /push/subscribe` (the write limiter, 8 KiB body, 422 on a bad subscription or hour, the bearer's player id on the row) and `POST /push/unsubscribe`; `DELETE /me` answers `pushSubscriptionsRemoved`; `/health` carries `push: { enabled, subscriptions, sent, removed, failed }`; main wires `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` / `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` / `VAPID_SUBJECT` (default the contact address) and `PUSH_FILE`, logs on or off, and runs `tick` every 60 s when on. `web-push` 3.6.7 is in `dependencies` (`@types/web-push` in dev), loaded on first use through a specifier the bundler does not see (`loadWebPush`), so `server/dist/verify.mjs` stays one file of 172 KB without it (the bundle test's 200 KB ceiling and the Docker-layout test hold) and the image's final stage runs `npm ci --omit=dev` for it; a verifier without keys never loads it; `npm audit` clean; the reason is in `server/README.md` next to the three endpoints; `deploy/verifier.env.example` and `deploy/README.md` carry the variables.

## Session 2026-10-30 (68) — production roadmap 0.10.3 installable web app (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- `game/public/manifest.webmanifest` (name, short name, `./` start and scope, standalone, orientation any, #1a1612 colours, icons 192 / 512 any + 512 maskable) and `game/public/icons/*.png` from `tools/make-icons.mjs` (the desktop icon's SVG through Chromium; the maskable one at 80 % on the tile colour); `index.html` gains the manifest link, theme-color, the Apple web-app metas and the touch icon.
- `game/sw.js` is a template: the build fills `const VERSION` (version+commit) and `const PRECACHE` (every file in `dist/` but the worker and `_headers`, sorted; `listFiles`, `renderServiceWorker` in `scripts/build-lib.mjs`). Install caches the shell; activate drops older caches and claims the page; navigations network-first with the cached page as the offline answer (any query, so the dev-API links work offline too); `build.json` network-first; everything else from this origin cache-first; other origins (our server) untouched; `skip-waiting` only on the page's message.
- `game/src/render/pwa.ts`: `register(dev)` (production only; `?sw=1` in dev; never on `file:`), the waiting worker → `updateReady` + `onUpdateReady`, `applyUpdate()` posts skip-waiting and reloads on `controllerchange` only when asked (the first install's claim must not reload a booting page), `checkForUpdate()`; the install side: the deferred `beforeinstallprompt`, `appinstalled`, `installOffer()` from the pure `shouldOfferInstall` (≥ 2 finished runs from `countRuns()`, not standalone or installed, a 14-day snooze after "Not now", 'prompt' when the browser offered one, 'ios' guidance on iPhone / iPad), `promptInstall()`, `snoozeInstall()`; state in `jd.install.v1`. `storage.ts` gains `readJson` / `writeJson` / `countRuns`.
- `ui/TitleScreen.ts`: a `notices` block under the top row: `update-ready` with `update-restart`, `install-offer` with `install` / `install-later` (or the iOS line); refreshed when a worker starts waiting or a prompt arrives while the title is up; the game scene never shows either. Strings `title.updateReady/updateRestart/installOffer/installBtn/installLater/installIOS`.
- `deploy/_headers`: `/sw.js` and `/manifest.webmanifest` no-cache, `/icons/*` a day (`tests/deploy.test.mjs` covers them and the non-overlap). `tests/hardening-e2e.test.mjs` blocks service workers in its routed contexts (Playwright's routes never see a worker's fetches). `tools/static-lib.mjs` is the loopback static server the first-interactive tool and the web-app e2e share.
- Tests: `tests/pwa.test.ts` (3: the manifest and the icons' real PNG sizes, the worker template and its rendering, the offer rule) and `tests/pwa-e2e.test.mjs` (4, against a production build on a loopback server: the worker active and controlling with the shell cached and the manifest and icons served; offline title in practice mode and a six-round practice game from the cache to its offline results; a second deploy's waiting worker unseen in the game scene, announced on the title, the restart rolling the cache to the new name; the install offer absent after one run, present after the second with a synthetic prompt event, quiet after "Not now", Install calling the prompt and recording the installation). Lighthouse's PWA category is gone since Lighthouse 12; the e2e's checks stand in for it.
- The preset D budget: CI's first measurement on 282c45b read p95 33.4 ms (106 of 706 frames dropped), a hair over the roadmap's 33: at 60 Hz a frame is 16.7 ms, so a p95 of exactly two frames is 33.4 and a budget of 33 cannot pass whenever the worst 5 % of frames are double frames. The gate now reads 34 (three frames, 50 ms, still fail); ⚑ the owner confirms or amends the roadmap's number.
- Verified: typecheck, lint, format:check and the strings lint clean; npm test: unit 225 pass (coverage 96.92 / 89.87 / 92.18), the two timing tests pass uninstrumented, browser 73 pass, 1 skipped (Firefox locally), 0 fail; the production build 291.0 KiB gzip (the manifest link and metas added a little), the web-app e2e 4 pass on its own too.
- GitHub on 282c45b (0.10.2): `ci` 41137856430 green (test, engines, image); `perf` 41137856323 **red** on the new preset D step only (p95 33.4 ms against 33, the two-frame reading explained above; the B gate passed, the bundle and first-interactive steps were skipped after the failure).
- Next: 0.10.4 push for the daily, opt-in (VAPID keys ⚑ the owner's, `/push/subscribe` and `/push/unsubscribe` on the verifier, one notification a day at the chosen hour, `web-push` as the first runtime dependency).

## Session 2026-10-30 (67) — production roadmap 0.10.2 weight and speed on weak phones (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- Where the weight was: the production bundle was 1.46 MB minified / 424 KiB gzip, 1.21 MB of it Phaser's `dist/phaser.esm.js`, one file esbuild cannot trim. `game/phaser-entry.cjs` builds Phaser from `phaser/src` with only the modules the game uses (factories, loader file types and scene plugins register themselves when required; the entry lists them explicitly); `scripts/esbuild-config.mjs` aliases `phaser` to it (so the dev server, the build and the hardening test share it), the `phaserFlagsPlugin` rewrites `typeof WEBGL_RENDERER` and friends in Phaser's source (webpack's DefinePlugin replaces the whole expression; esbuild's define cannot, and `typeof` of any defined value is a truthy string), `legalComments: 'none'` with one banner carrying Phaser's notice. Result: 984 KiB minified, 289.6 KiB gzip, 234 KiB brotli. Still in the bundle: the string table's translator notes (~7 KiB gzip) and the two JSON files that are also served from `public/` (~8 KiB): the next trims if 0.10.3's shell cache wants them.
- `tools/bundle-size.ts` (gzip level 9 of the bundle `build.json` names, `--max 300`), `tools/first-interactive.ts` (a static server over `dist/` with gzip for text, Chromium as a 412×915 @2.6 phone under CPU ×4 and Lighthouse's slow 4G: 1.6 Mbps / 750 Kbps / 150 ms; the time to the Title scene running with the daily button on the page; `--runs`, `--budget 4000`), `tools/perf-gate.ts --p95 <ms>` (an absolute budget every sample must meet, baseline or not; a sample without p95 fails it; `tests/perf-gate.test.ts` gains the case).
- `perf.yml`: after the B gate, preset D at 1× on the Canvas renderer (the runner's software WebGL is not a phone's GPU; the D WebGL number is not gated) against `--p95 33` plus the relative rules once `docs/baselines/ci/perf-ci-d.json` exists (⚑ commit it from a green run's artifact), then `npm run build` → the bundle weight → first interactive; the three JSON reports join the artifact.
- Local numbers: bundle 289.6 KiB gzip (PASS); first interactive 3489 / 3527 / 3533 ms over three runs, 319 KiB over 67 requests (PASS at 4000); preset D at 1× on Canvas locally: p50 16.7 ms, p95 16.8 ms, 3 dropped of 810 frames, script 2.43 ms, CPU 15.8 ms per frame (PASS at 33, `tools/scratch`).

## Session 2026-10-30 (65) — production roadmap 0.10.0 responsive layout (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- The fixed 1120×700 canvas is gone. `game/src/render/viewport.ts`: Phaser runs in `Scale.NONE` with `zoom: 1 / DPR`; a ResizeObserver on `#game` (the window's resize as a fallback) calls `game.scale.resize(w × DPR, h × DPR)` and sets the canvas's CSS size; `fitScreen(scene)` (title, results) zooms the camera by DPR and centres it on the container's CSS space, re-applied on every resize until shutdown. `render/dpr.ts` keeps only the DPR; `index.html` gives `#game` the full viewport (`100dvh`), the canvas `touch-action: none`.
- `game/src/render/layout.ts`: the board is the grid plus the shelf (`BOARD_W` 576 × `BOARD_H` 548 logical px); `fitBoard(slotW, slotH)` (centred, capped at `MAX_BOARD_SCALE` 1.25) and `boardCameraCenter` (the world point the camera centres on so the board lands in its slot) are pure; `binSlotXY` is shared by the scene and the geometry. `W`, `H`, `SIDE_X`, `SIDE_W` are gone.
- `game/src/ui/GamePanel.ts`: the shell is a CSS grid (HUD, the board slot `.jd-board`, the hint, the side panel). Wide: columns `minmax(0,1fr) clamp(17em, 32%, 26em)`, areas hud / board+side / hint+side, `max-width: 1400px` centred (1920×1080 gets margins, not scaling). Compact (`COMPACT_MQ`: under 700 px wide, or portrait under 1000): one column, the board's height from its width (capped at 52 dvh; 46 dvh on a portrait tablet so three offers show), the HUD without the title and the best line, the panel a bottom sheet (rounded top), the values legend a `<details>` closed by default, the Start row sticky at the sheet's bottom, the banner's foreman line wrapping. Short landscape (`LANDSCAPE_MQ`: under 520 px tall): two columns, the hint under the panel. The slot's rectangle goes to the scene through a ResizeObserver (`onBoard`, `relayout`); the scene answers with `setBoardVars(cell, dx, dy)` and `--px` (one board pixel in CSS px) is defined on the slot, so `em(px)` positions of the grid layer, the badges, the banner and the toast follow the drawn cells. The side panel's children keep `flex-shrink: 0` (the 44 px touch minimum replaces the content-based minimum, which would let the column squeeze the cards). Grid-area names and the media queries are built from single words so the strings lint does not read them as prose.
- `GameScene.layoutBoard(slot)`: `fitBoard`, the camera's zoom (DPR × scale) and centre, the panel's variables; `geometryInfo()` (the cell size, the grid and shelf rectangles, the shelf slots' centres in page CSS px) is exposed as `window.__jd.geometry()`. `TitleScene` lays the belt along the container's bottom edge (hidden under 520 px tall) and re-lays it on resize.
- `game/src/ui/dom.ts`: `#ui` covers the container (`--k` is gone); the base font size is `--base` by device class (16 px, 15 px under 1024 wide, 14 px under 600) times `--text-scale` and `--fit`; `--fit` only on screens 700 px and wider (a phone's title scrolls); screens centre their content with margins past 72 em; `@media (pointer: coarse)` gives buttons, offers, entries, links and the volume slider 44 px; the tips dialog's columns wrap and its demo canvas keeps its aspect under the dialog's width; the title's bottom strip (`jd-strip-head/left/right`) wraps under 900 px.
- Tests: `tests/layout.test.ts` (3: phone slots give 46–50 px cells, centring and the cap, the camera centre identity, the shelf slots); `tests/layout-e2e.test.mjs` (6 viewports at 130 %: title clipping and horizontal overflow, the draft's cell-size bounds and DOM-grid alignment, offers visible or reachable, the touch minimum under a coarse pointer, a real drag onto cell (3,4), the Start button on screen (reachable on the landscape phone), the banner inside the viewport and Next hittable, the results' horizontal overflow; screenshots `layout-<w>x<h>-{title,draft,build,summary,results}.png`). The fixture's `cellCenter` / `binSlotCenter` / `shelfPoint` are markers that `page.jd.at()` resolves against `__jd.geometry()`; `drag` takes them; `overlaySynced` checks the overlay and the canvas both cover the container. The HiDPI renderer test asserts the camera zoom as 2 × the board fit; the overlay test drops `--k`.
- Looked at: the matrix screenshots for all six viewports (the 390 and 412 phones: grid on top at 47–50 px cells, the shelf, the hint, the sheet with the foreman, offers and the collapsed legend; the Start row sticky; the banner over the top rows with Next; the results scroll with nothing sideways; the 768 tablet shows three offers; 1280 and 1920 keep the board beside the panel, 1920 with margins; 915×412 two columns with the hint under the panel). The in-app browser pane only advances frames while it is on screen, so the headless screenshots are the evidence.
- Verified: typecheck, lint, format:check and the strings lint clean; npm test: unit 218 pass (coverage 96.92 / 89.87 / 92.37), the two timing tests pass uninstrumented, browser 66 pass, 1 skipped (Firefox locally), 0 fail; the matrix file alone passes on all six viewports.
- GitHub on bdb50b6: `ci` 41129984258 green (test 13 min, engines 1 min, image 1 min); `perf` 41129984270 **failed the gate on preset B in both measurements**: Canvas dropped 79/113 and 80/114 frames (the baseline's rate is 28/173 + 15 %), WebGL script 20.45 then 18.01 ms (limit 19.31). Reproduced locally with `tools/perf.ts --device B`: before 0.10.0 Canvas p50 16.7 ms, 4 dropped of 197; on bdb50b6 p50 33.3 ms, 68 dropped of 124, CPU per frame 14.6 → 27.9 ms. Cause: at 1366×768 the board fitted at scale 1.135, so every sprite became a scaled blit (resampled on the Canvas renderer) and covered 29 % more pixels. Fix (the follow-up commit): `MAX_BOARD_SCALE` is 1 (a cell is never drawn larger than 72 px, which is also what "margins, not scaling" asks for at 1920) and the camera's scroll is rounded to whole pixels. Measured again locally: Canvas p50 16.7, 4 dropped of 197, CPU 15.6 ms (the baseline's 14.6); WebGL under the software renderer stays about a fifth costlier (CPU 25.8 vs 20.9 ms) because the canvas now covers the whole 1366×768 window instead of a 1120×700 box, inside the gate's 25 % script tolerance. If CI's WebGL sample still fails, the next step is to size the canvas to the board slot only (the page background already matches the canvas colour).
- Verified on the follow-up: typecheck, lint, format:check and the strings lint clean; tests/layout.test.ts 3 pass; layout-e2e 6/6, pseudo-e2e 3/3, render-e2e and game-e2e 36 pass, 0 fail.
- Next: 0.10.1 touch and the action table.

## v0.9 Clear — milestone review (2026-10-30, after 0.9.7)

The roadmap's exit criteria, checked against the code and the tests on 1ef18b0:

- **No banned word in the product**: `tests/strings.test.ts` scans every string in the table against the glossary's banned list (policy page excepted) and `tools/strings-lint.ts` finds no prose literal outside the table in `game/src/ui` and `game/src/content`; both run in `npm test` and are green. ✓
- **The foreman speaks on every screen**: the title (welcome, today's board, streak milestones), the draft (first pick, lesson steps, refusals, the bottleneck opener), the round-end banner and the results (first game, the band, offline); the once-only lines after the first share and the first crew. The modal panels (archive, crews, policy, privacy, your record) carry no line of his by design: they are lists and settings, not moments. ✓

## Session 2026-10-30 (63) — production roadmap 0.9.6 dead rounds: the study (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- `tools/dead-rounds.ts` (`--seeds 20 --tries 120 --prefix auto --policy greedy --json …`): the autoplayer plays the same seeds under rules v3 and under each candidate; per variant the flat-round total and its per-round spread, dead-end and mostly-flat seeds, score quantiles, replay verification under the variant's rules, the derived sanity bound, offers changed against the baseline, paired per-seed deltas, and the roadmap's gate (flat under 12 of 120, no seed worse, all verified, bound unchanged). The baseline reproduces v0.5's 29 of 120 exactly (median $1748, bound 20220).
- Candidates: the late-round offer guarantee (rounds 4–5: 25 flat, 0 worse / 5 better, +$39 mean, 13 drafts changed; rounds 4–6: 24, 0 / 6, +$37, 16 changed), slag $2 (29, no change), sell $12 (29, no change, bound 20232), furnace hold 2 (36 flat, median $2290, +$402: the line saturates a round sooner), hopper period 3 (36 flat, every seed worse, bound 13668), guarantee + slag (as the guarantee). On 40 seeds: baseline 57 of 240, guarantee 4–5 → 49 with 2 seeds worse, 4–6 → 47 with 2 worse, furnace 75, hopper 78. No candidate passes.
- The knob: `RULES.lateOfferGuarantee: { rounds: [], kinds: ['hopper', 'crusher'] }`; `offersFrom` swaps the last card of a listed round's draft that offers none of the kinds for one of them, drawn from the same stream (so unlisted rounds and already-fine drafts never change; the golden corpus and every replay are untouched while the list is empty). `tests/draft-guarantee.test.ts` pins both states over 200 seeds. `npm run policy` regenerated `game/public/policy.json` and `docs/POLICY.md` (the field shows, with a sentence saying it is off and measured). `tools/balance-lib.ts` exports `override()` for the study.
- Decision written in `docs/DESIGN.md` (decision log line and a section with the table): dead rounds are structural (a finished line on an 8×6 belt), rules v3 stays, recommendation no-go. ⚑ The owner's written go/no-go is the item's last acceptance; the study and the decision text are done.
- Verified: typecheck clean; `npm run lint` and `npm run format:check` clean; `npm test`: unit 211 pass with coverage 97.18 / 90.20 / 92.45 over the floors (the new draft-guarantee test included), browser 60 tests, 59 pass, 1 skipped (Firefox locally), 0 fail; the study's baseline reproduced the v0.5 autoplay figure (29 of 120) bit for bit.

## Session 2026-10-30 (62) — production roadmap 0.9.5 test suite, lint, coverage (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- `tests/e2e/fixture.mjs`: `fixture({ verifier, verifierEnv, browser })` starts `scripts/dev.mjs` and, when asked, `server/verify.ts` on free ports (`freePort()` through a throwaway listener) and launches one browser; `f.page(ctxOpts, { init })` gives a page with `errors` (page errors and console errors other than the offline fallback's refused connections), `ctx` and `jd` (the helpers: `api`, `frames(n)`, `when`, `title`, `draft`, `results`, `mode`, `draftRound`, `tick`, `textMatches`, `overlaySynced`, `settled` (no tween playing: Phaser's hit test misses a sprite that is still gliding into its cell, which is why a right-click 100 ms after a drop used to miss), `drag`, `dismissCopyDialog`, `noDialog`); `until(fn, { timeout, every, what })` polls; `settle(ms, why)` is the only sleep and demands a reason; `startVerifier({ port })` lets the offline test pick its port before the verifier exists. Helpers never shadow a Playwright method (the first draft put `context` and `title` on the page object and broke Playwright's own calls).
- Scenario suites: `tests/game-e2e.test.mjs` 19 tests in order on one page (boot and telemetry; first draft; pointer drag with the toast, the overlay and rotation; keyboard placement; the shift; save and continue; rounds 2 to 6; the checked submission and the card; share texts; exports; provenance; watching and the try; the minted daily and streaks; window, freeze, archive, weekly; the challenge link; crews; name moderation; keep-best; the daily button, the HUD at 130 % and the funnel), `tests/render-e2e.test.mjs` 16 independent tests each in its own context; foreman (3), pseudo-locale (3), offline (4), legal (5) likewise; hardening, deploy, control-room-live and desktop-smoke keep one test but lose their sleeps (a state wait, `until` on health, a tick wait, `settle` for the deliberate quiet period). The bot par poll is `until` with the same 120 s budget; the overlay-synced wait replaces the sleeps after a viewport change. The rotate check keeps one verified R and one verified right-click (after `settled()`) and restores the facing through the dev API: pressing keys as fast as the harness can, the two extra turns were the flaky part, not the product.
- `npm test` = `test:unit` (coverage over `game/src/sim/**` and `server/**`, floors lines 95 / branches 85 / functions 88; measured 97.6 / 90.2 / 92.4) then `test:e2e` (`tests/*-e2e.test.mjs`, deploy, control-room-live, desktop-smoke); `test:quick` is the unit glob without coverage. Instrumenting everything in one run made the suite three times slower and the par arrived after 117 s instead of 6; the split keeps the browser half clean.
- Lint and format: `eslint.config.mjs` (recommended sets, `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` with the `_` convention, `max-len` 160 ignoring strings, templates, URLs and comments, `no-empty` allowing the house `catch {}`, `prefer-const`, eslint-config-prettier), `.prettierrc.json` (160 columns, single quotes, trailing commas); `npm run lint`, `format`, `format:check`. ESLint's first pass: 332 findings, 304 of them line length (Prettier's job in the next commit); the other 28 fixed or justified in this commit. Dead code: `render/ui.ts` keeps `hoverColor`, `panel`, `label`. The verifier's "voided `rank`" named by the roadmap no longer exists as a dead field (`rank()` is live and used three times); nothing to remove there.
- `vendor/phaser` is out of Git (`.gitignore`), tsconfig keeps the fallback path; CLAUDE.md's sandbox note and CONTINUE.md step 2 carry the restore recipe (the ESM build, the types, the default-export shim, a package.json).

## Session 2026-10-30 (60) — production roadmap 0.9.3 onboarding delivery (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- Toasts (`GamePanel.setToast`, tid `toast`, kind `refusal` | `warning`): a refusal names the cell it was aimed at (the controller's reject hook passes the action's cell; the keyboard path the cursor's) and the toast sits above that cell (below it on the top row, clamped to the grid's width); no timer: the scene clears it on the next accepted action, on a new drag, when the shift starts; a ghost's warning replaces or clears a warning as the hand moves and goes when the pointer leaves the grid or Esc drops the part; after an accepted placement the scene re-checks the placed machine and keeps its warning at the cell. `rejectTimer` and the "Heads up:" hint are gone; the hint strip keeps the hint. `lastRejectText` stays for the dev API.
- The flow overlay (`GameScene.drawFlow`, a Graphics at depth 3, redrawn from `syncMachines`, cleared at run start and on the summary): for every placed machine and output direction, an amber chevron (alpha 0.5) at the shared edge when the neighbour's `inputTravelDirs` take that direction, else a red chevron and a bar; counts in `flowInfo()` for the dev API (`__jd.flow()`: visible, links, loose). Pure geometry from `sim/advice.ts`, the same answer the ghost's arrows and the warnings give; nothing is simulated.
- Textures: the from-behind-only machines (crusher ×2 frames, furnace, press ×3 frames, splitter) get a 14 px output arrow (was 9) and `notch()`: a dark slot with a blue "in" arrow on the back edge (the ghost's input blue).
- The lesson's round 3 (`tutorialHint`): `lesson.3place` (the splitter, shorter), `lesson.3top` and `lesson.3bottom` (a branch counts as back on the belt when a conveyor sits on the output cell, a conveyor where it points, and that one points into something that takes it), then `3flow`/`3noflow`; each step names a demo (`splitter`, `sideload`) and `GamePanel.setHint(text, demo)` adds the **Show me** button (tid `show-me`, `PanelActions.openTips(id)`). Telemetry steps unchanged (round 3 is step 5).
- The first draft: `content/firstpick.ts` `recommendFirstPick(offers)` ranks crusher, hopper, conveyor, splitter, press, furnace, tuner (sieve and dock last; never offered) and `hint.firstPick` says "First pick: {name} (card {n}) — {why}."; a newcomer reads `hint.newHere` (now without "Or dive in") before it.

## Session 2026-10-30 (59) — production roadmap 0.9.2 the results card (main, no rules change, protocol 2)

- The header is the card: `headline` (tid) leads with the standing sentence once our server has answered: five games or more "Better than {pct} % of {n} checked games {today | on this yard}" (`results.headBetter`), fewer "Rank {rank} of {n} checked games …" (`headRank`), without a checked entry of this game "{n} checked games … · submit yours to see where it stands" (`headUnchecked`), offline "Game complete · offline" (`headOffline`), while watching "Recording"; "Game complete" until then. Then the money, then the `deltas` row: `delta-par` against the foreman's mark (+{d} over / {d} under / exactly; green, dim, amber) once the mark is known, `delta-best` against this device's best on the yard before this game (`recordBest` now answers `prevSeedBest`, null on the first game there: "your first game on this yard" / +{d} over / {d} under ({best}) / equals), and the `medal` pill on a closed daily. On today's daily, when a daily of yesterday's date is in this device's bests, `since-yesterday` compares the two scores. The yard label, the personal-best line, the challenge line, the badges and Sal follow; the round bars (`round-bars`), what shipped, the board (chart with "#{rank} of {n} checked games" under it for five or more, the under-five line unchanged, the mark's line, the medals line, the neighbours, the top five entries) and the Play / Share / Watch rows stay below the fold. Nothing moved in the sim.
- The share text and the card carry a link by default (production roadmap: opt-out, not opt-in): the "Beat this" link of this game once it is checked (`challengeLink(verifiedHash)`), the yard's link before that; the checkbox reads **with link** and starts ticked; the tooltips say so.
